1	# RL Lab — Spec
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-18 · **Brief:** [brief.md](brief.md)
4	
5	## Summary
6	Three small 2D worlds, each with a ladder of hand-written learners. Classic Snake on a fixed 10×10 board is learned by table-based Q-learning, then a deep Q-network (a learned function that scores each move) built one piece at a time, then plain versus shaped rewards. A circular fish tank, where a bouncing ball removes any fish it touches, is learned by policy-gradient learners (which learn the chance of each move directly), built from REINFORCE up to PPO on one fish, then PPO with 5 fish sharing one learner, then a wide view versus the short view. In the same tank, a learning predator hunts 5 prey that forage for food pellets, and both sides learn with PPO up a ladder of fixes for a moving opponent: both learning at once, taking turns, then training against the other side's past versions, with a scripted-opponent control for each side and a tournament of each side's saved versions against the other's. Every learner is scored against a random player and a scripted bot on the same held-out games, every added piece gets a before-and-after comparison, every run saves its evidence, and any saved agent can be watched in a replay that shows how it chose its moves. See the [brief](brief.md) for why.
7	
8	## Users
9	| User | What they need to do |
10	|---|---|
11	| The learner (sole user) | Train each learner on Snake, in the fish tank or as predator and prey in under an hour, compare it with the baselines and with the previous rung of the ladder, explain each result from saved evidence, and watch any trained agent play |
12	
13	## Scope
14	**In scope:** Snake environment, Baselines, Table-based Q-learning, DQN ladder, Reward shaping, Training runs, Evaluation & comparison, Replay viewer, Fish tank environment, Policy-gradient ladder, Predator-prey environment, Co-learning ladder, Version tournament.
15	
16	**Out of scope:**
17	- 3D worlds.
18	- Learning from pixels or from a picture of the board or tank, and Atari games; agents see only the compact hand-made facts in SNAKE-06, TANK-11, HUNT-12 and HUNT-13. A grid-view Snake learner is a possible later follow-up.
19	- Reproducing research papers or chasing benchmark scores.
20	- Web deployment.
21	- Ready-made learning algorithms from libraries, multi-agent ones included; a comparison against one comes in a later stage.
22	- Evolutionary or genetic algorithms; co-learning here means two learners adapting to each other.
23	- A general framework beyond what Snake, the fish tank and predator-prey need.
24	- Changing the board or tank size, during training or between runs.
25	- Outside data of any kind.
26	- Stepping backwards in a replay.
27	- A separate learner for each fish of one kind: every fish in the fish tank shares one learner, and so does every prey.
28	- Shaped rewards outside Snake: the fish tank rewards only time survived, and predator-prey only pellets eaten and prey caught.
29	- In the fish tank, a ball that speeds up or more than one ball.
30	- Running every policy-gradient rung with both 1 and 5 fish; only PPO runs with 5 fish.
31	- In predator-prey, the policy-gradient rungs below PPO, two or more predators, and a predator that sees further than the prey.
32	
33	## Features
34	
35	### Snake environment (`SNAKE`)
36	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 5
37	**Stage:** S1
38	**Behavior:** Classic Snake on a grid. The snake moves one cell per move, turning left, turning right or going straight. Eating food makes it longer; hitting a wall or itself ends the game. The game hands the agent a short list of yes/no facts about its surroundings, and the reward for each move.
39	
40	**Requirements**
41	- **SNAKE-01** The game is played on a fixed 10×10 board of open cells surrounded by walls.
42	  - Given any game, when it starts, then the board has 10×10 open cells, walls on all four sides, a snake of length 3 in the middle row heading right, and exactly one food on an empty cell.
43	  - Given any training run or test game, when the board size is read, then it is 10×10.
44	- **SNAKE-02** On each move the agent chooses one of 3 moves relative to its heading: straight, turn left, turn right.
45	  - Given a snake heading right, when it turns left, then its head moves up one cell and its heading becomes up.
46	  - Given any heading, when any of the 3 moves is chosen, then the snake never reverses into its own neck.
47	- **SNAKE-03** Eating food makes the snake 1 cell longer, gives a reward of +1, and places a new food on an empty cell.
48	  - Given food directly ahead, when the snake moves straight, then its length goes up by 1, the move's plain reward is +1, and one new food appears on a cell not covered by the snake.
49	- **SNAKE-04** Moving into a wall or into the snake's own body ends the game with a reward of −1.
50	  - Given the head next to a wall, when the snake moves into the wall, then the game ends and the move's reward is −1.
51	  - Given the head next to a body cell that will still be occupied after the move, when the snake moves into it, then the game ends and the move's reward is −1.
52	- **SNAKE-05** Every move that neither eats nor kills has a plain reward of 0.
53	  - Given a move into an empty cell, when it is made, then its plain reward is 0.
54	- **SNAKE-06** At every move the agent sees exactly 11 yes/no facts: danger straight, danger left, danger right; heading up, down, left, right; food is up, down, left, right of the head.
55	  - Given the head next to the top wall while heading up, when the facts are read, then "danger straight" is yes and "heading up" is yes.
56	  - Given food above and to the left of the head, when the facts are read, then "food is up" and "food is left" are yes and "food is down" and "food is right" are no.
57	  - Given a cell occupied by the body or a wall next to the head on its left, when the facts are read, then "danger left" is yes.
58	- **SNAKE-07** A game ends after 100 moves in a row without eating.
59	  - Given a snake that has gone 100 moves since its last food (or since the start), when the 100th move is made without eating, then the game ends and that move's plain reward is 0.
60	- **SNAKE-08** The same game seed and the same moves always give the same game.
61	  - Given a game seed and a list of moves, when the game is played twice, then every board, food position, reward and ending is identical.
62	
63	**Edge cases & errors**
64	- The head moves into the cell the tail is leaving on that move → allowed; the game goes on.
65	- The snake fills every cell, so no food can be placed → the game ends as a full-board finish, with no −1.
66	- The food appears directly ahead of a turning snake → nothing special; food is only eaten when the head enters its cell.
67	
68	### Baselines (`BASE`)
69	**Serves:** Goal 2
70	**Stage:** S1
71	**Behavior:** Two non-learning players in each environment set the bar every learner must clear: one picks moves at random, the other follows a simple rule. In Snake the bot heads for the food; in the tank it flees the ball. In predator-prey each side has its own pair: the scripted chaser swims at the nearest prey it sees, and the scripted forager flees the predator, or else swims to the nearest pellet. All play the same test games as the learners.
72	
73	**Requirements**
74	- **BASE-01** The random player picks each move uniformly at random from the 3 moves.
75	  - Given 30,000 random-player moves, when the share of each move is counted, then each is between 32% and 35%.
76	- **BASE-02** The scripted bot moves toward the food, never making a move that kills it immediately when a safe move exists.
77	  - Given food to the left and the left cell free, when the bot moves, then it turns left.
78	  - Given food straight ahead and a wall straight ahead with the right cell free, when the bot moves, then it turns right.
79	  - Given several safe moves equally close to the food, when the bot moves, then it prefers straight, then left, then right.
80	- **BASE-03** When every move kills it, the scripted bot goes straight.
81	  - Given danger straight, left and right, when the bot moves, then it goes straight and the game ends.
82	
83	- **BASE-04** The random swimmer, used for the fish tank's fish, the prey and the predator, picks each move with a uniformly random direction and a speed drawn uniformly from 0 to its fish's top speed: 2.5 units per second for a tank fish or prey, 3.0 for the predator.
84	  - Given 10,000 random-swimmer moves, when their directions are sorted into four equal quarters of the circle, then each quarter holds between 23% and 27% of them.
85	  - Given 10,000 random-swimmer moves of a tank fish or prey, when their speeds are averaged, then the mean is between 1.2 and 1.3.
86	  - Given 10,000 random-predator moves, when their speeds are averaged, then the mean is between 1.45 and 1.55.
87	- **BASE-05** When the tank's scripted bot sees the ball, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly away from the ball.
88	  - Given the ball seen at relative position (1, 0), when the bot moves, then its swim velocity is (−2.5, 0).
89	- **BASE-06** When the tank's scripted bot doesn't see the ball, it stays still.
90	  - Given the ball seen as no, when the bot moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
91	- **BASE-07** In each comparison, the baselines play the same setup as the learner they are compared with: in the fish tank, the same fish count and view radius; in predator-prey, the same fixed opponent the learner is tested against (EVAL-01).
92	  - Given a 5-fish, wide-view learner, when its baselines are scored, then they play the same test tanks with 5 fish and the wide view.
93	  - Given a learning predator, when the random predator and the scripted chaser are scored, then they play the same test tanks against the scripted forager.
94	  - Given learning prey, when the random prey and the scripted forager are scored, then they play the same test tanks against the scripted chaser.
95	- **BASE-08** When the scripted chaser sees a prey, it swims at 3.0 units per second directly toward the nearest prey it sees.
96	  - Given the nearest prey seen at relative position (1, 0), when the chaser moves, then its swim velocity is (3.0, 0).
97	- **BASE-09** When the scripted chaser sees no prey, it stays still.
98	  - Given no prey seen, when the chaser moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
99	- **BASE-10** When the scripted forager sees the predator, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly away from the predator.
100	  - Given the predator seen at relative position (0, 2) and a pellet seen, when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (0, −2.5).
101	- **BASE-11** When the scripted forager doesn't see the predator but sees a pellet, it swims at 2.5 units per second directly toward the nearest pellet it sees.
102	  - Given the predator seen as no and the nearest pellet at relative position (−3, 0), when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (−2.5, 0).
103	- **BASE-12** When the scripted forager sees neither the predator nor a pellet, it stays still.
104	  - Given the predator and every pellet seen as no, when the forager moves, then its swim velocity is (0, 0).
105	
106	**Edge cases & errors**
107	- No safe move gets closer to the food → the bot takes the safe move that leaves it closest to the food, using the same preference order on ties.
108	- The tank's scripted bot flees into the wall → it stops at the wall (TANK-05) and keeps swimming away from the ball while it sees it.
109	- The scripted forager flees into the wall → it stops at the wall (HUNT-04) and keeps swimming away from the predator while it sees it.
110	- The scripted forager is less than 2.5 units from the nearest pellet → it reaches the pellet on the step it would pass it, at most 0.25 units of swimming.
111	
112	### Table-based Q-learning (`TAB`)
113	**Serves:** Goal 1
114	**Stage:** S1
115	**Behavior:** The first learner keeps a table with a score for each of the 3 moves in every combination of the 11 facts, and improves the table after every move from the reward and the best score of the next situation.
116	
117	**Requirements**
118	- **TAB-01** The table learner updates the score of the move it just made from the reward plus the discounted best score of the situation it reached (a one-step Q-learning update), after every move.
119	  - Given a known table, situation, move, reward and next situation, when one update is applied, then the new score equals the hand-computed Q-learning update for the run's learning rate and discount.
120	  - Given a move that ended the game, when the update is applied, then the next situation contributes nothing.
121	- **TAB-02** While training, the table learner sometimes picks a random move instead of its best one, at a rate that falls over the run from a starting rate to a floor saved with the run's settings.
122	  - Given a run's exploration settings, when the rate is read at the start, the middle and the end of training, then it matches the saved schedule at each point.
123	- **TAB-03** During test games the table learner always picks its highest-scored move.
124	  - Given a trained table, when a test game is played twice from the same seed, then both games are identical.
125	- **TAB-04** The learning update is written in the project; no ready-made learning algorithm is used.
126	  - Given the trained table learner, when its learning steps are traced, then each one is code written in the project.
127	
128	**Edge cases & errors**
129	- A situation never seen in training comes up in a test game → all 3 moves score equal and the learner picks by the order straight, left, right.
130	
131	### DQN ladder (`DQN`)
132	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 3
133	**Stage:** S1
134	**Behavior:** The second learner replaces the table with a small learned function that scores the 3 moves from the 11 facts. It is built in four rungs, each adding one piece to the one before, so each piece's effect can be measured on its own:
135	1. Plain: learns from each move as it happens.
136	2. + Replay buffer: learns from random samples of stored past moves.
137	3. + Target network: its learning targets come from a frozen copy of itself, refreshed at a fixed interval.
138	4. + Double DQN: the learner picks the best next move, and the frozen copy scores it.
139	
140	**Requirements**
141	- **DQN-01** The plain rung learns from each move right after it is made, using that move's reward plus the discounted best score of the next situation as its target.
142	  - Given a known function, move, reward and next situation, when one update is applied, then the target equals the hand-computed value and the function's score for that move moves toward it.
143	- **DQN-02** The replay-buffer rung stores past moves and learns from batches drawn at random from that store.
144	  - Given a store of past moves and a seed, when a batch is drawn, then every move in it comes from the store and the same seed draws the same batch.
145	  - Given a store at its saved capacity, when a new move is added, then the oldest move is dropped.
146	- **DQN-03** The target-network rung computes its targets from a frozen copy of the learner, refreshed every fixed number of updates saved with the run's settings.
147	  - Given the refresh interval, when updates run between two refreshes, then the copy does not change; when the interval is reached, the copy equals the learner.
148	- **DQN-04** The Double DQN rung chooses the next situation's best move with the learner, and scores that move with the frozen copy.
149	  - Given a learner and a copy that disagree on the next situation's best move, when the target is computed, then it uses the copy's score for the learner's chosen move.
150	- **DQN-05** Each rung differs from the rung before only by the piece it adds; every other setting is the same.
151	  - Given the saved settings of two neighbouring rungs, when they are compared, then the only differences are the added piece and its own settings.
152	- **DQN-06** While training, each rung explores with the same falling random-move rate as TAB-02; during test games it always picks its highest-scored move.
153	  - Given a trained rung, when a test game is played twice from the same seed, then both games are identical.
154	- **DQN-07** The learning updates of every rung are written in the project; no ready-made learning algorithm is used.
155	  - Given any trained rung, when its learning steps are traced, then each one is code written in the project.
156	
157	**Edge cases & errors**
158	- A move that ended the game is learned from → the next situation contributes nothing to its target.
159	- The replay-buffer store holds fewer moves than one batch → learning waits until it holds a full batch.
160	- Scores grow without bound (training diverges) → the run keeps going and saves its curve; the comparison reports the result, and the divergence is visible in the curve.
161	
162	### Reward shaping (`SHAPE`)
163	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 3
164	**Stage:** S1
165	**Behavior:** A second reward adds a small nudge toward the food on top of the plain reward, so the learner can be trained on either and the two compared.
166	
167	**Requirements**
168	- **SHAPE-01** The shaped reward is the plain reward plus 0.1 for a move that brings the head closer to the food and minus 0.1 for a move that takes it further away, measured in grid steps to the food that was on the board before the move.
169	  - Given a move that cuts the distance to the food from 5 to 4, when it is made, then the shaped reward is +0.1.
170	  - Given a move that raises the distance from 4 to 5, when it is made, then the shaped reward is −0.1.
171	  - Given a move that eats the food, when it is made, then the shaped reward is +1.1.
172	- **SHAPE-02** A move that ends the game by collision has a shaped reward of −1, with no nudge.
173	  - Given a move into a wall, when it is made, then the shaped reward is −1.
174	- **SHAPE-03** Every Snake run uses either the plain or the shaped reward for training, and saves which.
175	  - Given a finished Snake run, when its saved settings are read, then they name its reward.
176	- **SHAPE-04** Test scores never use the shaped reward; they always count food eaten.
177	  - Given the same agent tested once after plain training and once after shaped training, when the scores are read, then both are food eaten per game.
178	- **SHAPE-05** The plain-versus-shaped comparison trains the Double DQN rung on each reward, with every other setting the same.
179	  - Given the plain and shaped runs of that comparison, when their saved settings are compared, then the only difference is the reward.
180	
181	### Training runs (`RUN`)
182	**Serves:** Goal 3, Goal 5
183	**Stage:** S1
184	**Behavior:** One training run trains one learner on one environment setup from one seed. The learner starts the run with one action, watches its progress, and afterwards finds everything the run produced saved together, enough to recreate it.
185	
186	**Requirements**
187	- **RUN-01** A training run trains one learner setup from one training seed on one environment setup: Snake with one reward (the table learner or one DQN rung), the fish tank with one fish count and one view radius (one policy-gradient rung), or predator-prey with one ladder rung (both sides learning) or one control (one side learning).
188	  - Given a learner, an environment setup and a seed, when a run is started with one action, then it trains that learner on that setup with that seed and nothing else.
189	  - Given predator-prey rung 2 and a seed, when a run is started with one action, then it trains the predator and the prey together under that rung's rules and nothing else.
190	- **RUN-02** Each run saves its settings, seed, learning curve, final agent and test games.
191	  - Given a finished run, when its saved evidence is opened, then all five are there.
192	- **RUN-03** Rerunning from a run's saved settings and seed gives the same result.
193	  - Given a finished run, when it is run again on the same laptop from its saved settings and seed, then the test score at every curve point is identical.
194	- **RUN-04** While a run trains, it shows the steps trained so far, the latest test score of each learning side and the time elapsed.
195	  - Given a run in progress, when a curve point is reached, then the display updates all three.
196	  - Given a predator-prey ladder run in progress, when a curve point is reached, then the display shows both the predator's and the prey's latest test score.
197	- **RUN-05** A run never overwrites another run's evidence.
198	  - Given two runs with the same learner, reward and seed, when both finish, then both sets of evidence are kept.
199	- **RUN-06** A run that stops before it finishes is marked incomplete and is left out of every comparison.
200	  - Given a run stopped by hand partway through, when comparisons are made, then it is listed as incomplete and its numbers are not used.
201	- **RUN-07** A run still training at 60 minutes stops, saves what it has, and is marked over the time limit.
202	  - Given a run that reaches 60 minutes, when the limit passes, then training stops, its evidence so far is saved, it is marked over the time limit, and it is left out of every comparison.
203	- **RUN-08** Training, testing, saving evidence and replay work for a second environment without changes to them.
204	  - Given a minimal second environment used only for this check, when it is trained, tested and replayed, then none of the training, testing, saving or replay behavior had to change.
205	- **RUN-09** After each new environment is added, runs saved in the earlier environments still reproduce, compare and replay identically: Snake runs after the fish tank, and Snake and fish tank runs after predator-prey.
206	  - Given a Snake run saved before the tank was added, when it is rerun on the same laptop from its saved settings and seed, then the test score at every curve point is identical to the saved one.
207	  - Given the Snake comparisons and replays made before the tank was added, when they are made again, then every result and every replayed game is identical.
208	  - Given Snake and fish tank runs, comparisons and replays saved before predator-prey was added, when they are rerun or made again, then every curve point, result and replayed game is identical.
209	
210	**Edge cases & errors**
211	- A run is started with a learner, reward, fish count, view radius, rung or control that doesn't exist, or with a learner from another environment → it does not start, and the message lists the valid choices.
212	- The disk fills while saving → the run is marked incomplete and says which evidence is missing.
213	
214	### Evaluation & comparison (`EVAL`)
215	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
216	**Stage:** S1
217	**Behavior:** Every learner and baseline is scored on the same 100 held-out test games of its environment; in predator-prey each side plays a fixed scripted opponent, never its co-learning partner. Learning curves show progress against the baselines. Comparison reports say, from 5 training seeds per learner, whether each learner beats the baselines, and what each added piece changed. Each result carries a written explanation, so a tie counts.
218	
219	**Requirements**
220	- **EVAL-01** Every agent is scored on the same 100 test games of its environment setup, each started from a fixed test seed that is never used in training.
221	  - Given any two agents on the same environment setup, when they are tested, then they play games from the same 100 test seeds.
222	  - Given any training run, when its game seeds are compared with the test seeds, then none match.
223	  - Given a predator-prey run, when its predator is tested, then every test game pits it against the scripted forager; when its prey are tested, then every test game pits them against the scripted chaser.
224	- **EVAL-02** An agent's test score is the mean of its game scores over the 100 test games: food eaten per game in Snake, the game score of TANK-10 (mean seconds survived per fish) in the fish tank, and in predator-prey the side's score of HUNT-11 (prey caught per game for the predator, pellets eaten per prey for the prey).
225	  - Given 100 Snake test games with known food eaten, when the score is computed, then it equals their mean.
226	  - Given 100 tank test games with known game scores, when the score is computed, then it equals their mean.
227	  - Given 100 predator-prey test games with known catches, when the predator's score is computed, then it equals the mean catches per game.
228	- **EVAL-03** Each run's learning curve is its test score against steps trained, at 20 evenly spaced points, with the random player's and the scripted bot's scores drawn as lines on the same chart; the chart follows the `dataviz` skill.
229	  - Given a finished run, when its curve is opened, then it shows 20 test scores in order of steps trained, plus both baseline lines.
230	  - Given the 5 seeds of one learner, when their combined curve is opened, then it shows the mean across seeds at each point with the spread across seeds.
231	  - Given a finished predator-prey ladder run, when its curves are opened, then there is one curve for the predator and one for the prey, each with its own side's baseline lines.
232	- **EVAL-04** Each learner's final agent is compared with each baseline using the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill. It is given the 100 test-game scores of each of the learner's 5 seed runs, and the baseline's scores on the same 100 games. The learner beats the baseline when its score is higher and the difference is significant at 5% after correcting for the number of comparisons in the report; it ties when the difference is not significant; otherwise it loses.
233	  - Given 5 complete seed runs of a learner, when the comparison is made, then the report states beats, ties or loses against each baseline, with the test's result.
234	  - Given fewer than 5 complete seed runs, when the comparison is requested, then it is not made, and the report lists the missing seeds.
235	- **EVAL-05** Each added piece gets a before-and-after comparison with the rung below it, made the same way as EVAL-04. In Snake: table learner → plain DQN, plain → + replay buffer, + replay buffer → + target network, + target network → + Double DQN, and plain reward → shaped reward. In the tank: REINFORCE → + learned baseline, + learned baseline → actor-critic, actor-critic → PPO (all with 1 fish and the short view), 1-fish PPO → 5-fish PPO (short view), and 5-fish PPO short view → wide view. In predator-prey, for each side: its control → rung 1 (what a moving opponent costs), rung 1 → rung 2, and rung 2 → rung 3. Every outcome counts: better, worse, or no difference.
236	  - Given 5 complete seed runs of both rungs, when the comparison is made, then the report states better, worse or no difference, with the test's result and both rungs' combined curves on one chart.
237	- **EVAL-06** Every comparison result holds a written explanation; a result with no explanation is shown as unexplained.
238	  - Given a comparison result with an empty explanation, when the report is opened, then that result is marked unexplained.
239	  - Given a tie or a no-difference result with an explanation, when the report is opened, then it is marked explained.
240	- **EVAL-07** Each environment is done when its top learner beats the random player, beats or ties the scripted bot, every comparison in EVAL-04 and EVAL-05 for that environment has a result, and every tie and no-difference result is explained. Snake's top learner is the Double DQN agent; the fish tank's is 5-fish, short-view PPO. Predator-prey's top learners are both sides of rung 3, each judged against its own side's baselines, and predator-prey also needs every tournament chart (TOUR-03) to have a result, with rung 3's showing a trend or its no-trend result explained.
241	  - Given a Double DQN agent that beats both baselines and all comparisons made and explained, when the done check runs, then it reports done.
242	  - Given a Double DQN agent that ties the scripted bot with the tie explained, when the done check runs, then it reports done.
243	  - Given a tie with the scripted bot left unexplained, when the done check runs, then it reports not done and names the missing explanation.
244	  - Given a Double DQN agent that ties or loses to the random player, when the done check runs, then it reports not done.
245	  - Given 5-fish, short-view PPO that beats the random swimmer, ties the scripted bot with the tie explained, and every tank comparison made and explained, when the done check runs, then it reports the tank done.
246	  - Given 5-fish, wide-view PPO that beats the scripted bot while 5-fish, short-view PPO loses to it, when the done check runs, then it reports the tank not done.
247	  - Given a rung-3 predator that beats the random predator and ties the scripted chaser with the tie explained, rung-3 prey that beat the random prey and the scripted forager, every predator-prey comparison made and explained, and rung 3's tournament showing a trend, when the done check runs, then it reports predator-prey done.
248	  - Given everything else done but rung 3's tournament showing no trend and no explanation, when the done check runs, then it reports predator-prey not done and names the missing explanation.
249	- **EVAL-08** Each predator-prey control comparison states, beside its result, that the control trained against the same scripted opponent it is tested against.
250	  - Given the predator control → rung 1 comparison, when the report is opened, then it says the control trained against the scripted forager, its test opponent.
251	  - Given the prey control → rung 1 comparison, when the report is opened, then it says the control trained against the scripted chaser, its test opponent.
252	
253	**Edge cases & errors**
254	- Every seed of a learner scores exactly the same as a baseline → the result is a tie.
255	- A run marked incomplete or over the time limit is among a learner's runs → it is left out, and the comparison waits for 5 complete runs.
256	- A predator-prey control run → it gives only its learning side's agent; the side that played the scripted bot has no agent to compare.
257	
258	### Replay viewer (`REPLAY`)
259	**Serves:** Goal 3, Goal 4, Goal 5
260	**Stage:** S1
261	**Behavior:** The learner picks a saved agent and one of its test games, or a fresh game seed, and watches it play step by step. Next to the board or tank, the viewer shows how the agent chose: its score for each of Snake's 3 moves, or each fish's chosen swim and how sure it was, predator included, so its choices can be explained. In predator-prey, any saved predator can be watched against any saved prey, or against the other side's scripted bot.
262	
263	**Requirements**
264	- **REPLAY-01** Any saved test game of any saved agent, the baselines included, can be watched as a replay.
265	  - Given a finished run, when one of its test games is chosen, then the replay plays it from the first move to the end.
266	- **REPLAY-02** A Snake replay shows the board, the snake, the food, the move number, the food eaten so far, and how the game ended.
267	  - Given a replay at any move, when it is shown, then all of these are visible; at the end, the ending (wall, body, 100 moves without food, full board) is shown.
268	- **REPLAY-03** At every move of a Snake learner's game, the replay shows the agent's score for each of the 3 moves, with the chosen move marked.
269	  - Given a table learner or DQN replay at any move, when it is shown, then 3 scores are visible and the chosen one is marked.
270	  - Given a baseline replay, when it is shown, then the move scores are shown as not applicable.
271	- **REPLAY-04** The replay can be paused, resumed, and stepped forward one step at a time.
272	  - Given a paused replay, when "step" is chosen, then it advances exactly one step and stays paused.
273	- **REPLAY-05** The replay speed can be set from 1 to 30 steps per second.
274	  - Given a speed of 5 steps per second, when it plays for 10 seconds, then it advances 50 steps (±2).
275	- **REPLAY-06** A replay of a saved test game is identical to the game that was scored.
276	  - Given a saved test game, when it is replayed, then every state, move and score matches the saved game.
277	- **REPLAY-07** A saved agent can play and be watched on a fresh game from any seed chosen at the time.
278	  - Given a saved agent and a new seed, when the game is watched twice, then both games are identical.
279	- **REPLAY-08** A tank replay shows the tank, the ball, every fish still swimming, the time in seconds, the number of fish still swimming, and the time each removed fish was removed.
280	  - Given a tank replay at any step, when it is shown, then all of these are visible; at the end, the ending (every fish removed, or the 60-second cap) is shown.
281	- **REPLAY-09** At every step of a fish tank or predator-prey learner's game, each learning fish still swimming, the predator included, shows an arrow for its chosen swim velocity and a number for how sure it was: the spread of its move choice, smaller meaning surer.
282	  - Given a policy-gradient replay at any step, when it is shown, then every learning fish still swimming has an arrow and a spread number.
283	  - Given a tank baseline replay, or a scripted side in a predator-prey replay, when it is shown, then its arrows and spreads are shown as not applicable.
284	- **REPLAY-10** A predator-prey replay shows the tank, the predator, every prey still swimming, the pellets, the time in seconds, the pellets eaten so far, the prey caught so far, and the time each caught prey was caught.
285	  - Given a predator-prey replay at any step, when it is shown, then all of these are visible; at the end, the ending (every prey caught, or the 60-second cap) is shown.
286	- **REPLAY-11** A fresh predator-prey game can pair any saved predator with any saved prey, or either one with the other side's scripted bot.
287	  - Given a predator saved by one run, prey saved by another run and a seed, when the game is watched twice, then both games are identical.
288	
289	**Edge cases & errors**
290	- The chosen agent or game can't be found or read → the viewer opens nothing and names the missing item.
291	- A game ends on its first move → the replay shows that one move and the ending.
292	
293	### Fish tank environment (`TANK`)
294	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 5
295	**Stage:** S2
296	**Source:** [ideas/fish-tank.md](ideas/fish-tank.md)
297	**Behavior:** A circular tank holds one ball, bouncing off the wall at constant speed, and 1 or 5 fish. Every tenth of a second each fish still swimming chooses how to swim. The ball touching a fish removes it; the wall and other fish are harmless. Each fish sees only what lies within its view radius, as a short list of numbers, and is rewarded for every step it survives.
298	
299	**Requirements**
300	- **TANK-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 holding one ball and a fixed number of fish, 1 or 5, saved with the run.
301	  - Given any tank game, when it starts, then the tank's radius is 10, it holds exactly one ball, and the number of fish is the run's 1 or 5.
302	- **TANK-02** The ball, of radius 0.5, moves at a constant 2 units per second and bounces off the tank wall like a mirror.
303	  - Given the ball reaching the wall, when it bounces, then it leaves at the same angle to the wall's normal as it arrived, on the other side of the normal.
304	  - Given any step of any game, when the ball's speed is measured, then it is 2 units per second.
305	- **TANK-03** Each game starts with the ball at the centre heading in a direction set by the game seed, and each fish at a seed-chosen spot between 5 units from the centre and the wall.
306	  - Given any game, when it starts, then the ball is at the centre and every fish is between 5 and 9.75 units from the centre.
307	  - Given the same game seed twice, when both games start, then the ball's direction and every fish's spot are identical.
308	- **TANK-04** Every 0.1 seconds of tank time, each fish still swimming chooses a move: a swim velocity (x, y) in tank directions, capped at a speed of 2.5 units per second.
309	  - Given a fish that chooses velocity (1, 0), when the step runs, then it moves 0.1 units in the x direction.
310	  - Given a fish that chooses velocity (3, 4), when the step runs, then it swims at 2.5 units per second in direction (0.6, 0.8) and moves 0.25 units.
311	- **TANK-05** A fish, of radius 0.25, can't leave the tank: a move that would carry it past the wall stops it at the wall.
312	  - Given a fish touching the wall that swims straight outward, when the step runs, then it stays inside the tank, touching the wall.
313	- **TANK-06** Fish swim through each other unharmed.
314	  - Given two fish whose paths cross, when the step runs, then both finish their moves and both are still swimming.
315	- **TANK-07** At the end of each step, the ball removes every fish whose edge touches it (centres within 0.75 units); a removed fish takes no more moves and is no longer seen by other fish.
316	  - Given a fish whose centre ends a step 0.7 units from the ball's centre, when the step ends, then the fish is removed.
317	  - Given a fish whose centre ends a step 0.8 units from the ball's centre, when the step ends, then the fish is still swimming.
318	  - Given a removed fish, when the other fish's views are read, then none of them sees it.
319	- **TANK-08** A tank game ends when every fish is removed, or at 60 seconds of tank time (600 steps); until then it goes on for the fish still swimming.
320	  - Given 5 fish of which 4 are removed, when the steps continue, then the game goes on until the last fish is removed or 600 steps have run.
321	  - Given a fish still swimming after 600 steps, when the 600th step ends, then the game ends.
322	- **TANK-09** A fish's reward is +0.1 for each step it survives, and 0 for the step in which it is removed, which ends its experience; there is no other reward.
323	  - Given a fish that survives a step, when the step ends, then its reward is +0.1.
324	  - Given a fish removed during a step, when the step ends, then its reward is 0 and it has no further steps.
325	  - Given a fish that survives all 600 steps, when its rewards are summed, then the total is 60.
326	- **TANK-10** A fish's score is the seconds of tank time it survived, from 0 to 60, and a game's score is the mean over all its fish, removed ones included.
327	  - Given 5 fish that survive 10, 20, 30, 60 and 60 seconds, when the game is scored, then its score is 36.
328	  - Given 1 fish removed at 12.5 seconds, when the game is scored, then its score is 12.5.
329	  - Given a fish removed at the end of step k, when it is scored, then it survived 0.1·k seconds, while its TANK-09 rewards sum to 0.1·(k−1), since the removal step pays 0: removed at the end of step 125, it scores 12.5 and its rewards sum to 12.4.
330	- **TANK-11** At every step, each fish sees exactly 11 numbers about what lies within its view radius: for the ball, whether it is seen, its position relative to the fish (2 numbers) and its velocity relative to the fish (2); for the wall, whether it is seen and the offset from the fish to the wall's nearest point (2); for the nearest other fish, whether it is seen and its position relative to the fish (2). Anything beyond the radius is seen as no, and its numbers are 0.
331	  - Given the short view and the ball 3 units away, when the fish's view is read, then the ball is seen as yes and its relative position and velocity are the true ones.
332	  - Given the short view and the ball 5 units away, when the view is read, then the ball is seen as no and its 4 numbers are 0.
333	  - Given the short view and a fish at the centre of the tank, when the view is read, then the wall is seen as no and its 2 numbers are 0.
334	  - Given a 1-fish game, when the view is read, then the nearest other fish is seen as no and its 2 numbers are 0.
335	- **TANK-12** A fish's view radius is either 4 units (the short view) or 20 units (the wide view, which spans the whole tank), saved with the run.
336	  - Given the wide view, when any fish's view is read at any step, then the ball and the wall are seen as yes, and so is the nearest other fish if one is still swimming.
337	- **TANK-13** The same game seed and the same moves always give the same game.
338	  - Given a game seed and a list of moves for every fish, when the game is played twice, then every position, removal, reward and ending is identical.
339	
340	**Edge cases & errors**
341	- Two other fish are exactly equally near → the fish sees the one with the lower fish number.
342	- The ball touches several fish in the same step → all of them are removed in that step.
343	- A fish chooses velocity (0, 0) → it stays where it is.
344	- A fish starts too close to the ball to survive → can't happen: fish start at least 5 units from the ball.
345	
346	### Policy-gradient ladder (`PG`)
347	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 3
348	**Stage:** S2
349	**Source:** [ideas/fish-tank.md](ideas/fish-tank.md)
350	**Behavior:** The tank's learners keep a policy: a learned function that turns a fish's 11 numbers into a spread of swim velocities to pick from. Learning makes the moves that led to longer survival more likely. The ladder is built in four rungs on one fish, each adding one piece to the one before, so each piece's effect can be measured on its own:
351	1. REINFORCE: after each game, each move is made more likely in proportion to the return (the discounted rewards) that followed it.
352	2. + Learned baseline: a learned estimate of each situation's return is subtracted first, so each move is pushed by how much better or worse it did than expected.
353	3. Actor-critic: the return is replaced by the reward plus the discounted estimate of the next situation, so learning happens every few steps instead of waiting for the game to end.
354	4. PPO: each batch of experience is learned from several times, with each update clipped so the policy can't move far from the one that collected it.
355	
356	Then PPO trains 5 fish sharing one policy, and then 5 fish with the wide view.
357	
358	**Requirements**
359	- **PG-01** The REINFORCE rung, after each game, raises the chance of each move a fish made in proportion to the discounted return that followed it.
360	  - Given a known policy, a finished game, the run's learning rate and discount, when one update is applied, then the change to the policy equals the hand-computed REINFORCE step.
361	- **PG-02** The learned-baseline rung weights each move by its return minus a learned estimate of the return from the situation it was made in, and trains that estimate toward the returns seen.
362	  - Given a move with return 4.0 from a situation estimated at 3.0, when its weight is computed, then the weight is 1.0.
363	  - Given a situation whose estimate is below the returns seen from it, when the estimate is trained, then the estimate rises toward them.
364	- **PG-03** The actor-critic rung weights each move by its reward plus the discounted estimate of the next situation, minus the estimate of the situation it was made in, and learns after every fixed number of steps saved with the run's settings.
365	  - Given a known reward, discount and two estimates, when a move's weight is computed, then it equals the hand-computed value.
366	  - Given the run's steps per update, when training runs, then an update happens after every that many steps, not only at the end of a game.
367	- **PG-04** The PPO rung learns from each batch of experience in a fixed number of passes, and caps each move's change in chance at a clip range, both saved with the run's settings.
368	  - Given a move whose chance has already risen past 1 + the clip range times its chance when collected, and a positive weight, when the update is computed, then that move pushes the policy no further.
369	  - Given the run's passes per batch, when one batch is learned from, then the number of passes equals it.
370	- **PG-05** Each tank setup differs from the one it is compared with only by the piece it adds: one rung from the rung below, 5-fish PPO from 1-fish PPO only by the fish count, and wide-view PPO from short-view PPO only by the view radius.
371	  - Given the saved settings of any two setups compared in EVAL-05, when they are compared, then the only differences are the added piece and its own settings.
372	- **PG-06** With several fish on one side, the fish tank's 5 fish or predator-prey's 5 prey, every fish on that side uses the same policy on its own view, and every one's experience trains it.
373	  - Given a 5-fish run, when moves are chosen, then every fish's move comes from the one policy applied to that fish's own 11 numbers.
374	  - Given a 5-fish run, when an update is applied, then it learns from the experience of all 5 fish up to each one's removal.
375	  - Given a predator-prey run with the prey learning, when an update is applied, then the prey policy learns from all 5 prey up to each one's capture.
376	- **PG-07** While training, each learning fish, the predator included, picks its move at random from its policy's spread; during test games it takes the policy's most likely move.
377	  - Given a trained policy, when a test game is played twice from the same seed, then both games are identical.
378	- **PG-08** The learning updates of every rung, and of both predator-prey sides, are written in the project; no ready-made learning algorithm is used.
379	  - Given any trained rung or predator-prey side, when its learning steps are traced, then each one is code written in the project.
380	
381	**Edge cases & errors**
382	- A picked velocity is faster than 2.5 units per second → the fish swims at the cap (TANK-04), and the learner learns from the move it picked.
383	- A fish is removed partway through a stretch of steps → its experience ends there, and the situation after its removal contributes nothing.
384	- The policy's spread collapses or learning diverges → the run keeps going and saves its curve; the comparison reports the result, and the problem is visible in the curve.
385	
386	### Predator-prey environment (`HUNT`)
387	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 5
388	**Stage:** S3
389	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
390	**Behavior:** The fish tank's circle, with a learning predator in place of the ball, 5 prey and 10 food pellets. Every tenth of a second the predator and each prey still swimming choose how to swim. A prey touching a pellet eats it, and the pellet reappears elsewhere; the predator touching a prey removes it. Everyone sees only what lies within the short view radius. Prey are rewarded for pellets, the predator for catches.
391	
392	**Requirements**
393	- **HUNT-01** The tank is a circle of radius 10 holding one predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets.
394	  - Given any game, when it starts, then the tank's radius is 10 and it holds exactly one predator, 5 prey and 10 pellets.
395	  - Given any step of any game, when the pellets are counted, then there are 10.
396	- **HUNT-02** Each game starts with the predator at the centre, each prey at a seed-chosen spot between 5 units from the centre and the wall, and each pellet at a seed-chosen spot at least 2 units from every prey.
397	  - Given any game, when it starts, then the predator is at the centre, every prey is between 5 and 9.75 units from the centre, and every pellet is at least 2 units from every prey.
398	  - Given the same game seed twice, when both games start, then every prey and pellet spot is identical.
399	- **HUNT-03** Every 0.1 seconds of tank time, the predator and each prey still swimming choose a swim velocity as in TANK-04, capped at 3.0 units per second for the predator and 2.5 for a prey.
400	  - Given the predator choosing velocity (3, 4), when the step runs, then it swims at 3.0 units per second in direction (0.6, 0.8) and moves 0.3 units.
401	  - Given a prey choosing velocity (3, 4), when the step runs, then it swims at 2.5 units per second and moves 0.25 units.
402	- **HUNT-04** No fish leaves the tank: a move that would carry the predator (radius 0.5) or a prey (radius 0.25) past the wall stops it at the wall.
403	  - Given the predator touching the wall and swimming straight outward, when the step runs, then it stays inside the tank, touching the wall.
404	- **HUNT-05** Prey swim through each other unharmed, and the predator swims through pellets without effect.
405	  - Given two prey whose paths cross, when the step runs, then both finish their moves and both are still swimming.
406	  - Given the predator passing over a pellet, when the step ends, then the pellet is still there.
407	- **HUNT-06** At the end of each step, the predator catches every prey whose centre is within 0.75 units of its own; a caught prey is removed, takes no more moves and is no longer seen.
408	  - Given a prey whose centre ends a step 0.7 units from the predator's, when the step ends, then it is caught.
409	  - Given a prey whose centre ends a step 0.8 units from the predator's, when the step ends, then it is still swimming.
410	  - Given a caught prey, when the other fish's views are read, then none of them sees it.
411	- **HUNT-07** At the end of each step, after catches, each prey still swimming eats every pellet whose centre is within 0.5 units of its own, and each eaten pellet reappears at once at a seed-chosen spot at least 2 units from every prey still swimming.
412	  - Given a prey whose centre ends a step 0.4 units from a pellet, when the step ends, then the pellet is eaten, a new pellet stands at least 2 units from every prey, and there are still 10 pellets.
413	  - Given a prey 0.6 units from a pellet, when the step ends, then the pellet is not eaten.
414	- **HUNT-08** A game ends when all 5 prey are caught, or at 60 seconds of tank time (600 steps).
415	  - Given 4 prey caught, when the steps continue, then the game goes on until the last prey is caught or 600 steps have run.
416	- **HUNT-09** A prey's reward for a step is +1 for each pellet it eats in that step and 0 otherwise; the step in which it is caught pays 0 and ends its experience.
417	  - Given a prey that eats one pellet in a step, when the step ends, then its reward is +1.
418	  - Given a prey caught during a step, when the step ends, then its reward is 0 and it has no further steps.
419	- **HUNT-10** The predator's reward for a step is +1 for each prey it catches in that step and 0 otherwise.
420	  - Given the predator catching 2 prey in one step, when the step ends, then its reward is +2.
421	- **HUNT-11** A game's prey score is the pellets eaten per prey: all pellets eaten by the 5 prey, caught ones included, divided by 5. Its predator score is the number of prey caught, from 0 to 5.
422	  - Given 5 prey that eat 3, 0, 5, 2 and 0 pellets, when the game is scored, then the prey score is 2.0.
423	  - Given 3 prey caught by the end, when the game is scored, then the predator score is 3.
424	- **HUNT-12** At every step, each prey sees exactly 14 numbers about what lies within its view radius: for the predator, whether it is seen, its position relative to the prey (2 numbers) and its velocity relative to the prey (2); for the wall, whether it is seen and the offset to its nearest point (2); for the nearest other prey, whether it is seen and its relative position (2); for the nearest pellet, whether it is seen and its relative position (2). Anything beyond the radius is seen as no, and its numbers are 0.
425	  - Given the predator 3 units from a prey, when the prey's view is read, then the predator is seen as yes and its relative position and velocity are the true ones.
426	  - Given the predator 5 units from a prey, when the prey's view is read, then the predator is seen as no and its 4 numbers are 0.
427	- **HUNT-13** At every step, the predator sees exactly 11 numbers about what lies within its view radius: for the wall, whether it is seen and the offset to its nearest point (2); for the nearest prey, whether it is seen, its relative position (2) and its relative velocity (2); for the nearest pellet, whether it is seen and its relative position (2). Anything beyond the radius is seen as no, and its numbers are 0.
428	  - Given the nearest prey 3 units from the predator, when the predator's view is read, then it is seen as yes with its true relative position and velocity.
429	  - Given every prey caught but the game not yet over, when the predator's view is read, then the nearest prey is seen as no and its 4 numbers are 0.
430	- **HUNT-14** The predator and every prey have the same view radius: 4 units, the short view of TANK-12.
431	  - Given a predator and a prey 3.9 units apart, when both views are read, then each sees the other; at 4.1 units apart, neither does.
432	- **HUNT-15** The same game seed and the same moves always give the same game.
433	  - Given a game seed and a list of moves for the predator and every prey, when the game is played twice, then every position, catch, pellet, reward and ending is identical.
434	
435	**Edge cases & errors**
436	- Two prey or two pellets are exactly equally near → the fish sees the one with the lower number.
437	- A prey is caught in the step it reaches a pellet → catches are settled first, so it is removed and eats nothing.
438	- Two prey are within 0.5 units of the same pellet → the lower-numbered prey eats it.
439	- The predator catches several prey in one step → all are removed, and its reward counts each one.
440	
441	### Co-learning ladder (`COEV`)
442	**Serves:** Goal 1, Goal 3
443	**Stage:** S3
444	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
445	**Behavior:** The prey share one PPO policy, as the fish tank's 5 fish did; the predator has its own. Because each side's opponent changes while it learns, the ladder adds one fix for a moving opponent per rung, so each fix's effect can be measured:
446	1. Both learn at once: plain co-learning, expected to chase or cycle.
447	2. Take turns: one side is frozen while the other trains, swapping every 20 updates.
448	3. Past versions: each side trains against a pool of the other side's saved past versions.
449	
450	Two controls each train one side against the other side's scripted bot, to measure what a moving opponent costs that side. Three rungs and two controls make 5 setups.
451	
452	**Requirements**
453	- **COEV-01** The predator has its own policy, which learns with the PPO update of PG-04 from the predator's experience only.
454	  - Given a ladder run, when the predator's policy is updated, then it learns only from the predator's moves and rewards.
455	- **COEV-02** In rung 1, both policies learn from the same games at every update.
456	  - Given a rung-1 run, when any update is applied, then both the predator's and the prey's policies change.
457	- **COEV-03** In rung 2, one side learns while the other is frozen, swapping every 20 updates of the learning side, starting with the predator.
458	  - Given a rung-2 run, when its first 20 updates run, then only the predator's policy changes; during the next 20, only the prey's does.
459	- **COEV-04** In rung 3, each side saves a copy of its policy every 20 of its updates into a pool that keeps its last 10 copies, and each training game pits a learning side against a copy drawn uniformly at random from the other side's pool, its latest copy included; the drawn copy does not learn.
460	  - Given a side that has saved 11 copies, when its pool is read, then it holds the 10 latest.
461	  - Given a pool of 10 copies and 10,000 draws, when the draws are counted, then each copy is drawn between 9% and 11% of the time.
462	- **COEV-05** Each control trains one side against the other side's scripted bot: the predator against the scripted forager, or the prey against the scripted chaser, with every other setting the same as rung 1. Both controls are run, so predator-prey has 5 setups (3 rungs, 2 controls) and 25 runs at 5 seeds each.
463	  - Given a predator control run, when it trains, then every prey move comes from the scripted forager and only the predator's policy changes.
464	  - Given a prey control run, when it trains, then every predator move comes from the scripted chaser and only the prey's policy changes.
465	- **COEV-06** Each predator-prey setup differs from the one it is compared with only by the piece it adds: rung 2 from rung 1 by taking turns, rung 3 from rung 2 by the pool, and rung 1 from each control by the learning opponent.
466	  - Given the saved settings of any two setups compared in EVAL-05, when they are compared, then the only differences are the added piece and its own settings.
467	
468	**Edge cases & errors**
469	- Rung 3 starts before a side has saved a copy → that side's pool holds its starting policy.
470	- One side stops winning anything (the predator never catches, or the prey are always caught at once) → the run keeps going and saves its curves; the comparison reports the result, and the problem is visible in the curves and the tournament.
471	
472	### Version tournament (`TOUR`)
473	**Serves:** Goal 2, Goal 3
474	**Stage:** S3
475	**Source:** [ideas/predator-prey.md](ideas/predator-prey.md)
476	**Behavior:** Each ladder run saves versions of both sides as it trains. Every predator version then plays every prey version on held-out tanks, and one chart per rung shows how catches change as each side gets older, so the learner can see whether later predators beat earlier prey (and the other way round), or whether the two sides cycle.
477	
478	**Requirements**
479	- **TOUR-01** Each ladder run (rungs 1 to 3) saves 10 versions of each side at evenly spaced points through training, the last at the end.
480	  - Given a finished rung-2 run, when its saved versions are listed, then there are 10 predator and 10 prey versions, the last of each equal to the final agent.
481	- **TOUR-02** Every pairing of a run's predator version i with its prey version j, for i and j from 1 to 10, plays the same 20 held-out test tanks, and is scored as the predator's catches per game (HUNT-11).
482	  - Given a finished ladder run, when its tournament is played, then all 100 pairings have a score, each from the same 20 test seeds, none of them used in training.
483	- **TOUR-03** Each rung has one tournament chart: a 10 × 10 grid of predator version against prey version, each cell the mean over the rung's 5 seed runs of that pairing's score; the chart follows the `dataviz` skill.
484	  - Given 5 complete seed runs of a rung, when its chart is opened, then it shows 100 cells, each the mean of 5 scores.
485	  - Given fewer than 5 complete seed runs of a rung, when its chart is requested, then it is not made, and the report lists the missing seeds.
486	- **TOUR-04** Each rung's tournament is tested for a trend with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill. It is given the 100 pairing scores of each of the rung's 5 seed runs. The tournament shows a trend when catches rise with the predator's version and fall with the prey's version, each significant at 5% after correcting for the number of tests in the report; otherwise it shows no trend, which, like a tie, needs a written explanation (EVAL-06), such as a cycle.
487	  - Given scores that rise with predator version and fall with prey version in every seed, when the test runs, then the chart reports a trend, with the test's result.
488	  - Given scores that cycle, when the test runs, then the chart reports no trend and is marked unexplained until its explanation is written.
489	
490	## Data requirements
491	- **DATA-01** All training and test experience comes from the agents playing in the lab's environments, Snake, the fish tank and predator-prey; no outside data is used.
492	  - Given any run, when its inputs are listed, then the only source of games is the run's own environment, started from the run's own seeds.
493	
494	## Quality requirements
495	- **QR-01** Each training run in any environment, including its test games, finishes in under 1 hour (60 minutes) on the laptop's processor; a predator-prey run trains both its learning sides within that hour, and its tournament games are not part of the run.
496	  - Checked by the elapsed time saved with each run; RUN-07 stops any run that reaches 60 minutes.
497	- **QR-02** Training, testing and replay run on the Apple Silicon laptop with no NVIDIA graphics card.
498	  - Checked by running every learner's training, a comparison and a replay on that laptop.
499	
500	## Assumptions
501	- One learner uses the lab on one Apple Silicon laptop, evenings and weekends.
502	- Each environment's full ladder takes many hours in total; only each single run has the 1-hour limit. Snake and the fish tank have 6 learner setups × 5 seeds = 30 runs each: Snake's are the table learner, 4 DQN rungs and the shaped-reward Double DQN; the tank's are the 4 policy-gradient rungs with 1 fish, PPO with 5 fish, and 5-fish PPO with the wide view. Predator-prey has 5 setups × 5 seeds = 25 runs: 3 ladder rungs and 2 controls.
503	- The done check in EVAL-07 judges only each environment's top learner; the other rungs are reported, and may lose to the scripted bot, but don't gate it.
504	- Predator-prey's lower rungs may lose, chase or cycle; that is part of the lesson, as long as it is explained.
505	- Learner tuning settings (learning rate, discount, exploration schedule, store size, refresh interval, steps per update, passes per batch, clip range, the policy's starting spread, each predator-prey side's training length) are saved per run but not fixed by the spec; they are found during the build within the one-hour limit.
506

7	### core.ActionSpace: type
8	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class ActionSpace: kind: Literal["discrete", "continuous"]; size: int; names: tuple[str, ...]; max_norm: float | None; score_names: tuple[str, ...]`
9	- **Invariants:** `discrete`: `size` moves named by `names`, a move is an `int` in `[0, size)`, `max_norm` is `None`; `continuous`: a move is a float32 vector of length `size`, its components named by `names`, its length capped at `max_norm` by the environment; `score_names` names the numbers `Agent.scores` gives per agent (Snake: `straight, left, right`; the tank: `spread`) (AD-11)

13	### core.Step: type
14	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Step: obs: np.ndarray; rewards: np.ndarray; acting: np.ndarray; ended: np.ndarray; done: bool; score: float; ending: str | None`
15	- **Invariants:** `obs` float32 `(n_agents, obs_size)`, rows of agents not acting all 0; `rewards` float `(n_agents,)`, 0 for agents that didn't act; `acting` bool, who moves next; `ended` bool, whose experience ended on this step (removed, killed, or the game ended); `done` exactly when no agent acts; `score` is the game score so far (Snake: food eaten; the tank: TANK-10's mean seconds survived); `ending` is `None` until `done`, then an environment-defined name (Snake: `wall`, `body`, `starved`, `full_board`; the tank: `all_removed`, `time_cap`)
16	- **Boundary:** snake, tank, evaluate, train, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Step/ · **Cases:** an ordinary Snake move; a food move; a wall death; a tank step removing 1 of 5 fish; a tank step reaching the cap

19	### core.Transition: type
20	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Transition: agent: int; obs: np.ndarray; action: int | np.ndarray; reward: float; next_obs: np.ndarray; done: bool`
21	- **Invariants:** one agent's step; `obs` and `next_obs` float32 `(obs_size,)`; `action` an `int` for discrete moves, the float32 vector the agent chose (before any cap) for continuous ones; `done` is that agent's `Step.ended`, and when `done` every learner ignores `next_obs`
22	- **Boundary:** train, tabular, dqn, pg · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Transition/ · **Cases:** a mid-game Snake move; a game-ending Snake move; a tank fish's move; a tank fish's removal

26	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Shape: kind: Literal["cell", "circle", "ring"]; x: float; y: float; r: float; style: str; agent: int | None`
27	- **Invariants:** world units; `cell` is the unit square with corner `(x, y)`, `r` unused; `circle` filled and `ring` outlined, centred at `(x, y)` with radius `r`; `style` names a colour (`head`, `body`, `food`, `tank`, `ball`, `fish`; unknown styles get a fallback); `agent` is set on the shape an agent's arrow starts from (Snake's head, each fish)

29	- **Used by:** snake, tank, replay

31	### core.Frame: type
32	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Frame: bounds: tuple[float, float, float, float]; y_down: bool; shapes: tuple[Shape, ...]; labels: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]`
33	- **Invariants:** `bounds` is `(x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max)` and every shape lies inside it; `y_down` when y grows downward (Snake: `(0, 0, 10, 10)`, true; the tank: `(-10, -10, 10, 10)`, false); shapes draw in order; `labels` are name/value lines (Snake: food eaten; the tank: time in seconds, fish swimming, each removed fish's removal time) (REPLAY-02, REPLAY-08)

37	### core.Env: type
38	`class Env(Protocol): name: str; n_agents: int; obs_size: int; actions: ActionSpace; def reset(self, seed: int) -> np.ndarray; def step(self, moves: np.ndarray) -> Step; def frame(self) -> Frame`
39	- **Invariants:** `reset` returns float32 `(n_agents, obs_size)`, every agent acting; `moves` is int64 `(n_agents,)` for discrete moves or float32 `(n_agents, size)` for continuous ones, and rows of agents not acting are ignored; same seed and moves → identical `Step`s and `Frame`s (AD-11)
40	- **Errors:** `ValueError` for a discrete move outside `[0, size)`, a non-finite continuous move, or the wrong shape; `RuntimeError` for `step` before `reset` or after `done`
41	- **Boundary:** registry, evaluate, train, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Env/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a recorded 20-move Snake game from seed 7; one of a recorded 30-step 5-fish tank game from seed 7
42	- **Used by:** registry, evaluate, train, replay; PL-01, PL-03
43	
44	### core.Agent: type
45	`class Agent(Protocol): name: str; def act(self, obs: np.ndarray, *, explore: bool, rng: np.random.Generator) -> np.ndarray; def scores(self, obs: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray | None`
46	- **Invariants:** one move per `obs` row, each row decided on its own (PG-06), shaped as `Env.step` takes them; with `explore=False` a value learner picks its highest score, ties by move order (straight, left, right), and a policy learner its most likely move; `scores` is `(rows, len(score_names))`, or `None` for baselines
47	- **Boundary:** registry, evaluate, train, replay · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Agent/ · **Cases:** a replay double of the scripted bot over a recorded 20-move game; one of a table learner with scores; one of a PPO policy over a recorded 5-fish game
48	- **Used by:** registry, evaluate, train, replay; PL-01, PL-03

50	### core.Learner: type
51	`class Learner(Agent, Protocol): def observe(self, t: Transition, step: int, rng: np.random.Generator) -> None; def settings(self) -> dict[str, int | float | str]; def save(self, path: Path) -> None; @classmethod def load(cls, path: Path) -> Learner`
52	- **Invariants:** `observe` is the only place learning happens, called once per acting agent per step, in agent order; `act(explore=True)` is the training behaviour (value learners: `Explore.rate(step)`; policy learners: a sample from the policy); a loaded learner acts exactly like the saved one
53	- **Errors:** `load` raises `FileNotFoundError` or `ValueError` for a missing or unreadable file
54	- **Boundary:** registry, train, store · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Learner/ · **Cases:** a replay double of a table learner over 50 recorded transitions; one of a plain DQN rung; one of a REINFORCE rung over a recorded 1-fish game
55	- **Used by:** registry, train, store; PL-01
56	
57	### core.Explore: type
58	`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Explore: start: float; floor: float; decay_steps: int` with `def rate(self, step: int) -> float`
59	- **Invariants:** falls linearly from `start` at step 0 to `floor` at `decay_steps`, then stays; `1 ≥ start ≥ floor ≥ 0` (TAB-02, DQN-06)
60	- **Boundary:** tabular, dqn · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/core.Explore/ · **Cases:** 1.0 → 0.05 over 100,000 steps, read at 0, 50,000 and 200,000

81	`class TankEnv(Env): def __init__(self, fish: Literal[1, 5] = 1, view: Literal["short", "wide"] = "short") -> None`
