  "content": "# RL Lab — Architecture\n\n**Status:** Draft · **Shape:** Single package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-18 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)\n\n## Overview\n`rllab` is one Python package, run from the command line. It holds three environments, Snake, the fish tank and predator-prey, on one shared core. Every environment is one or more sides of agents stepped together: Snake's one snake, the tank's school of fish, and predator-prey's predator and five prey. A training run builds one environment setup, an agent for each side it trains, and a schedule. The schedule decides, game by game and step by step, which agent plays each side and which sides learn: Snake and the tank always have their one side learning, while predator-prey's ladder rungs and controls follow the co-learning rules. At each of 20 curve points the run pauses training and plays 100 reserved test games greedily for each side that learns, each against its side's fixed test opponents, then writes the point to the run's folder. A predator-prey ladder run also saves 10 versions of each side as it goes. When the run finishes it saves its agents and test games, and writes the run's status last. A separate tournament command plays every saved predator version against every saved prey version. Comparisons read only complete runs and their tournaments, write one report per environment, and hand the per-game scores to the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill. The replay viewer rebuilds any saved game, or a fresh pairing of saved agents, by stepping the environment through its moves. It draws Snake's move scores, or each learning fish's swim arrow and spread. Training, testing, storage, the tournament, comparison and replay see only the `core` protocols and the registry, never an environment, a learner or a schedule (AD-04, AD-11, AD-16).\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  cli --> train & tournament & compare & replay\n  train --> registry & evaluate & store\n  tournament --> registry & evaluate & store\n  registry --> snake & tank & hunt & baselines & tabular & dqn & pg & colearn\n  baselines --> snake & tank & hunt\n  replay --> registry & evaluate & store\n  compare --> store\n  store --> evaluate\n  snake & tank & hunt & baselines & tabular & dqn & pg & colearn & evaluate & store --> core\n```\n\n## Tech stack\n| Area | Choice | Decision |\n|---|---|---|\n| Language | Python 3.12, managed with uv | AD-01 |\n| Neural networks | PyTorch ≥ 2.4, CPU only, one thread | AD-02, AD-05 |\n| Numerics and tank simulations | NumPy ≥ 2.0 | AD-05, AD-13, AD-18 |\n| Run storage | Folder per run: JSON, NumPy `.npz`, PyTorch `.pt`, JSON Lines; tournament grids as JSON | AD-06, AD-19 |\n| Statistics | SciPy ≥ 1.13, used through the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill | AD-08 |\n| Charts | Matplotlib ≥ 3.9, PNG, following the `dataviz` skill | AD-09 |\n| Replay window | pygame-ce ≥ 2.5 | AD-07 |\n| CLI | `argparse`, one `rllab` entry point | — |\n| Tests | pytest | — |\n\n## Components\n| Component | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `core` | Protocols and types every environment, agent and schedule speaks: `Side`, `Env`, `Agent`, `Learner`, `Schedule`, `Lineup`, `ActionSpace`, `Step`, `Transition`, `Frame`, `Shape`, and the falling exploration rate | RUN-08, DATA-01, TAB-02, DQN-06 | — |\n| `snake` | The 10×10 Snake game: moves, food, endings, the 11 facts, plain and shaped rewards, seeded play | SNAKE-01–SNAKE-08, SHAPE-01, SHAPE-02 | `core` |\n| `tank` | The circular fish tank: bouncing ball, 1 or 5 fish swimming continuously, removals, the 11 view numbers within a short or wide view, survival reward and score, seeded play | TANK-01–TANK-13 | `core` |\n| `hunt` | Predator-prey in the same circular tank: one predator and 5 prey swimming continuously, 10 respawning pellets, catches, the predator's 11 and each prey's 14 view numbers, pellet and catch rewards, both sides' scores, seeded play | HUNT-01–HUNT-15 | `core` |\n| `baselines` | Snake's random player and food-seeking bot; the random swimmer for any fish at its top speed; the tank's fleeing bot; predator-prey's scripted chaser and scripted forager; all behind `Agent` | BASE-01–BASE-06, BASE-08–BASE-12 | `core`, `snake`, `tank`, `hunt` |\n| `tabular` | Table-based Q-learner behind `Learner`, its update hand-written | TAB-01, TAB-03, TAB-04 | `core` |\n| `dqn` | One DQN learner whose rung (plain, replay buffer, target network, Double DQN) is a setting; every update hand-written | DQN-01–DQN-07 | `core` |\n| `pg` | One policy-gradient learner over continuous moves whose rung (REINFORCE, learned baseline, actor-critic, PPO) is a setting; one policy for every fish of a side, and a separate one for each side; every update hand-written | PG-01–PG-04, PG-06–PG-08, COEV-01 | `core` |\n| `colearn` | The schedules behind `Schedule`: one side always learning, and predator-prey's rungs (both at once, taking turns, past-version pools) and controls; each side's pool of past copies | COEV-02–COEV-05 | `core` |\n| `registry` | Names the environments, setups, learners, baselines and schedules of each environment; builds an environment, a run's agents and schedule, and each side's test lineup from settings; loads a saved agent or version | RUN-01, BASE-07 | `snake`, `tank`, `hunt`, `baselines`, `tabular`, `dqn`, `pg`, `colearn` |\n| `evaluate` | The 100 reserved test seeds; plays greedy test games of a full lineup and scores the tested side by its game score | EVAL-01, EVAL-02, SHAPE-04 | `core` |\n| `store` | Run folders: settings, curve, agents, versions, test games, status; tournament grids; never overwrites; lists runs by environment, learner, setup and status | RUN-02, RUN-03, RUN-05, RUN-06, SHAPE-03 | `core`, `evaluate` |\n| `train` | The training loop: one environment setup, one seed, the run's agents and schedule; every acting agent's moves to its side's learner while that side learns; each learning side's curve points and versions, progress line, 60-minute stop, interrupt handling | RUN-01, RUN-04, RUN-06, RUN-07, PG-06, TOUR-01, QR-01, QR-02 | `registry`, `evaluate`, `store` |\n| `tournament` | Plays every predator version of a complete ladder run against every prey version of the same run on 20 held-out tanks and saves the grid | TOUR-02 | `registry`, `evaluate`, `store` |\n| `compare` | One report per environment: learning-curve and tournament charts, baseline and ladder comparisons, tournament trend tests, settings-difference checks, control notes, explanations, the done check. Runs the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill for each comparison and trend test, and the `dataviz` skill for each chart | EVAL-03–EVAL-08, BASE-07, DQN-05, SHAPE-05, PG-05, COEV-06, TOUR-03, TOUR-04 | `store` |\n| `replay` | Rebuilds a saved game, a fresh game or a fresh pairing of saved agents step by step and shows it in a window: Snake's move scores, or each learning fish's swim arrow and spread; pause, step and speed | REPLAY-01–REPLAY-11 | `registry`, `evaluate`, `store` |\n| `cli` | The `rllab` commands: `train` (learners and baselines, any environment), `tournament`, `compare`, `replay`; rejects unknown names with the valid choices | RUN-01 | `train`, `tournament`, `compare`, `replay`, `registry` |\n\n**Layers (UR-65).** The shared core is `core` plus the runners `evaluate`, `store`, `train`, `tournament`, `compare`, `replay` and `cli`. `registry` is the one seam between that core and the parts that differ per environment. Import-linter enforces these layers, top to bottom:\n\n| Layer | Components | May import |\n|---|---|---|\n| Entry | `cli` | everything below |\n| Runners (shared core) | `train`, `tournament`, `compare`, `replay`, `store`, `evaluate` | `registry`, `core`, each other as the Components table says |\n| Seam | `registry` | environments, baselines, learners, `colearn`, `core` |\n| Baselines | `baselines` | environments' observation enums, `core` |\n| Environments · Learners · Schedules | `snake`, `tank`, `hunt` · `tabular`, `dqn`, `pg` · `colearn` | `core` only; a learner never imports an environment, and no environment imports another |\n\n## Interfaces\nSee [packages/rllab/contract.md](packages/rllab/contract.md).\n\n## Data\n- **Experience (DATA-01):** every game is an environment from `registry.make_env`, started from a seed. In each environment, seeds 0–99 are the test seeds (`evaluate.test_agent`), and the tournament plays seeds 0–19 of them (AD-19). Training game seeds are drawn from the run seed's generator in `[1_000, 2^31)`, so they never meet a test seed (AD-05).\n- **Run evidence (RUN-02):** each run gets `runs/<env>/<learner>/<setup>/seed-<n>/<UTC timestamp>-<short id>/`, which holds:\n  - `settings.json`\n  - `curve.json`, with one score per learning side at each point\n  - `agent-<side>.npz` (table) or `agent-<side>.pt` (DQN, policy gradient) for each side the run trains\n  - `versions/<side>-<k>.pt` for k 1–10 in a predator-prey ladder run (TOUR-01)\n  - `test_games.jsonl`: each tested side's 100 games, with the side, the seed, each step's moves for every agent of every side, per-agent move scores or spreads, every side's game score after each step, and the ending\n  - `status.json`, written last (AD-06)\n\n  `<setup>` is `plain` or `shaped` in Snake, `fish-<1|5>-<short|wide>` in the tank, and the match (`rung-1`, `rung-2`, `rung-3`, `control-predator`, `control-prey`) in predator-prey. Baselines are stored the same way, with no curve:\n  - Snake's under the setup `none`\n  - the tank's under the fish count and view they were scored with\n  - predator-prey's under the side they play (`predator` or `prey`), against the other side's scripted bot (BASE-07)\n- **Tournaments (TOUR-02):** `tournaments/hunt/pg-ppo/<rung>/seed-<n>/<run id>.json` holds one ladder run's 10 × 10 grid, written once and never overwritten (AD-19).\n- **Explanations (EVAL-06):** the learner writes one Markdown file per comparison or tournament, `reports/<env>/explanations/<comparison id>.md`. `compare` reads them and never writes them.\n- **Reports (EVAL-03–EVAL-05, TOUR-03):** `reports/<env>/report.md` and its PNG charts, one report per environment, rebuilt from the runs and tournaments on every `rllab compare` (AD-14).\n\n## Cross-cutting concerns\n- **Reproducibility (RUN-03, RUN-09, SNAKE-08, TANK-13, HUNT-15):** every random draw comes from a `np.random.Generator` or `torch.Generator` derived from the run seed. PyTorch runs on the CPU with one thread and deterministic algorithms (AD-05). Adding an environment changes no code path, seed stream, setup label or file format of the earlier ones, so their saved runs rerun, compare (AD-14) and replay identically. `hunt` shares no simulation code with `tank` (AD-18).\n- **Time (QR-01, RUN-07):** `train` checks the wall clock after every step and at every test game. At 60 minutes it stops and saves what it has. Elapsed seconds are saved with each curve point. A step is one environment step, whatever the number of sides or agents (AD-11, AD-16). Tournament games run outside the run, under their own command (AD-19).\n- **Errors:** `UnknownName` with the valid choices is raised for an unknown environment, setup value, match, side or learner, or for a learner from another environment. A disk error while saving marks the run incomplete and lists the missing evidence. Ctrl-C marks the run incomplete.\n- **Testing:** pytest. RUN-08 is checked with a test-only second environment, a tiny grid walk in `tests/`, which is trained, tested, stored and replayed through the same code. RUN-09 is checked against Snake runs saved before the tank lands, and against Snake and tank runs saved before predator-prey lands.\n\n## Project layout\n```\nsrc/rllab/   → core/ snake/ tank/ hunt/ baselines/ tabular/ dqn/ pg/ colearn/ registry/ evaluate/ store/ train/ tournament/ compare/ replay/ cli/ — one folder per component; __init__ exports its contract\nruns/        → run evidence (git-ignored)\ntournaments/ → tournament grids (git-ignored)\nreports/     → snake/, tank/ and hunt/: report, charts, explanations/ (explanations committed)\ntests/       → folders only; files are planned in pipelines.md\n```\n\n## Decisions\n### AD-01: Python 3.12 in one package, `rllab`\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** One learner, one laptop, PyTorch basics already known (brief). The lab needs one install and no separate deployments; every environment trains, compares and replays through the same commands.\n- **Decision:** Python 3.12 managed with uv; a single package `rllab` with one folder per component and one `rllab` command. The shared core is kept apart by the import layers (Components), not by a package boundary.\n- **Alternatives:** Python 3.13: PyTorch and pygame-ce wheels for Apple Silicon lag on new versions. Several packages (core, envs, agents): no separate install or deployment boundary (AD-04); UR-34: \"Keep one package despite the contract length warning\"; UR-65: \"Keeping it in the single package with a clearly separated shared-core component is fine\".\n- **Consequences:** `requires-python = \">=3.12,<3.13\"`. `core` and the runners are the seam that would become their own package if environments multiply.\n\n### AD-02: Hand-written learners on PyTorch, CPU only\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** UR-07: the user chose \"(a) Write DQN myself in NumPy/PyTorch\". UR-32: \"PyTorch is fine for AD-02\", over NumPy with hand-written backpropagation. TAB-04, DQN-07 and PG-08 forbid ready-made learning algorithms. QR-02: an Apple Silicon laptop, no NVIDIA card. The networks are tiny (11 or 14 inputs, a handful of outputs).\n- **Decision:** The table learner is NumPy. Every network is a small `torch.nn` MLP whose learning rule (targets, returns, advantages, losses, batch sampling, target refresh, clipping) is written in the learner's own component; `dqn` (AD-03) and `pg` (AD-12) set their networks. PyTorch supplies only tensors, autograd and the Adam optimizer; `torch.distributions` is not used. No RL library, multi-agent ones included, is imported anywhere. Everything runs on the CPU; the Apple GPU (MPS) is not used.\n- **Alternatives:** NumPy with hand-written backpropagation: you'd learn the gradient maths too, but the backward pass, not the RL, becomes the work. Stable-Baselines3 or similar: ruled out by DQN-07, PG-08 and UR-07. MPS: slower than the CPU for networks this small, and nondeterministic.\n- **Consequences:** Autograd hides the gradients but not the learning rule. Import-linter forbids RL libraries and `torch.distributions`.\n\n### AD-03: One DQN learner, the rung a setting\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** dqn\n- **Context:** UR-06: Snake is \"Good first env for DQN-style value learning\". DQN-01–DQN-04 build four rungs. DQN-05 and SHAPE-05 require neighbouring rungs to differ only by the added piece.\n- **Decision:** `dqn.DQNAgent` takes a `DQNSettings` whose `rung` is `plain`, `replay`, `target` or `double`, with a `torch.nn` MLP of 11 → 64 → 64 → 3. Each rung turns on exactly one more piece: a replay store sampled in batches, a frozen copy refreshed every `target_every` updates, Double DQN target selection. The plain rung trains on each move as a batch of one. All other fields share defaults, and `compare.build_report` refuses a pair whose saved settings differ anywhere else.\n- **Alternatives:** Four separate classes: duplicated code, so one rung's change can leak into another unseen. A single class with independent on/off flags: allows combinations the ladder never compares.\n- **Consequences:** A rung's added piece is one code branch, easy to read and test against its hand-computed target. Later variants (prioritised replay, say) add a rung value.\n\n### AD-04: A shared core reused across environments\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** UR-16: \"a shared core reused across environments: env interface, training loop, logging, replay viewer\". UR-22: \"build it through Snake, shaped by Snake's needs, and adjust it later\". UR-53: \"reuse the shared core (env interface, training loop, logging, replay viewer) from Snake\". RUN-08 requires a second environment to need no changes to training, testing, saving or replay. The brief rules out a general framework built ahead of need.\n- **Decision:** Environments implement `core.Env`; agents implement `core.Agent`, and learners `core.Learner`. The runners (`train`, `evaluate`, `store`, `tournament`, `compare`, `replay`) import only `core`, `registry` and each other, never an environment, a learner or a schedule; those come from `registry`. `core` holds only what the lab's environments use; AD-11 and AD-16 set its shape. A test-only grid walk proves RUN-08.\n- **Alternatives:** Environment-specific runners: RUN-08 would fail, and each new environment would copy the loop. A Gymnasium-style interface: adds spaces, wrappers and render modes beyond what the lab's environments need, and Gymnasium itself brings dependencies the lab doesn't use.\n- **Consequences:** A new environment that needs something `core` lacks (image observations, say) changes `core`, as UR-22 expects. Import-linter enforces the layers.\n\n### AD-05: Seeds, test-seed reservation and CPU determinism\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** RUN-03 needs identical curves on a rerun. SNAKE-08, TANK-13, HUNT-15, TAB-03, DQN-06, PG-07 and REPLAY-07 need repeatable games. EVAL-01 needs test seeds never used in training. RUN-09 needs each environment's draws untouched by the ones added after it. COEV-04 draws opponents at random.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **Streams:** a run seed builds a `np.random.SeedSequence`. Its first child gives training game seeds. Each side then gets three children of its own: move choice (exploration or sampling from the policy), batch sampling, and network initialisation (a `torch.Generator`). One last child gives opponent draws (AD-17). Children are spawned in that order, side by side in `Env.sides` order, so a one-side run's streams don't depend on what other environments use.\n  - **Seed ranges:** test seeds are 0–99; training game seeds are drawn from `[1_000, 2^31)`.\n  - **Torch:** runs with `torch.set_num_threads(1)` and `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`.\n  - **Per game:** each game owns its own generator from its game seed, so food placement, the ball's start direction, every fish's and pellet's start spot and every pellet's respawn depend only on the seed and the moves.\n- **Alternatives:** A global seed (`np.random.seed`): any new random call shifts every later draw. One shared move-choice stream for both sides: a change to one side's acting would shift the other's draws. Several threads: faster on big networks, but the reduction order varies between runs.\n- **Consequences:** Results reproduce on the same laptop and library versions, not across machines; `settings.json` records the versions. One thread stays fast enough for these networks (Risks).\n\n### AD-06: One folder per run, status written last\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** RUN-02 lists the evidence and TOUR-01 adds versions. RUN-05 forbids overwriting. RUN-06 and RUN-07 mark runs incomplete or over time and keep them out of comparisons. In the disk-full edge case, the run must name the missing evidence.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **Start:** `store.begin_run` creates a new folder `runs/<env>/<learner>/<setup>/seed-<n>/` whose name ends with a UTC timestamp and a short random id. It writes `status.json` as `running` first.\n  - **During the run:** curve points and versions are written as they come.\n  - **Finish:** `finish` writes each trained side's agent and the test games, then rewrites `status.json` with the final status and any missing files.\n  - **Writes:** every file is written to a temporary name and renamed. Test-game moves are written as JSON numbers: integers for discrete moves, and float32 values as the exact float64 they convert to, so they read back bit for bit.\n  - **Reading back:** `list_runs` treats `running` with no live process as incomplete.\n- **Alternatives:** SQLite: queryable, but a run's evidence is no longer a folder you can open and read. MLflow or Weights & Biases: extra servers or accounts for one learner on one laptop.\n- **Consequences:** Comparisons scan folders, which is instant for about 90 runs. Evidence is plain JSON you can open; agents and versions are `.npz` or `.pt`. A tank or predator-prey run's saved test games come to a few megabytes.\n\n### AD-07: Replay in a pygame window, rebuilt from saved moves\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** replay\n- **Context:** REPLAY-02–REPLAY-05 and REPLAY-08–REPLAY-10 need:\n  - the board or tank\n  - Snake's move scores, or each learning fish's swim arrow and spread\n  - pause, step, and 1–30 steps per second\n\n  REPLAY-06 needs the replay to match the scored game. REPLAY-11 pairs agents saved by different runs. Web deployment is out of scope.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **Rebuild:** `replay` rebuilds each frame by resetting the environment to the saved seed and stepping through the saved moves. A fresh game or pairing plays its lineup greedily from the chosen seed. `ReplayMismatch` is raised if a step's game score or the ending differs from the saved game.\n  - **Window:** each `core.Frame`'s shapes (cells, circles, rings) are drawn in a pygame-ce window, scaled from the frame's bounds, with the frame's labels beside it. Space pauses and resumes, the right arrow steps, and the up and down arrows change speed.\n  - **What each side shows:** this follows that side's `core.ActionSpace`. Discrete moves get one bar per move score, with the chosen move marked. Continuous moves get an arrow from each acting agent's shape along its chosen move, plus the agent's numbers (the tank and predator-prey: spread). A side played by a baseline shows \"n/a\".\n- **Alternatives:** A Matplotlib animation: its widgets can't reliably hold 30 frames a second with controls. A self-contained HTML player: needs a JS front end to maintain, closer to the web work the brief leaves out. A terminal (curses) view: can't draw a circular tank.\n- **Consequences:** Saved games are small (moves only). The viewer draws any `Frame` and any side's `ActionSpace` kind, so a new environment replays unchanged (RUN-08).\n\n### AD-08: Comparisons through the null-hypothesis-testing skill; explanations are the learner's files\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** compare\n- **Context:** EVAL-04, EVAL-05 and TOUR-04 require the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill on each seed run's scores, at 5% after correcting for the number of tests in the report. EVAL-06 and EVAL-07 need a written explanation for each result. BASE-07 scores a learner against baselines run on its own setup.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **What the skill gets:** every test in `compare` hands the skill the per-seed scores of what it compares, alpha 0.05, and the number of tests in that environment's report (AD-14). For a comparison, those are both compared agents' per-game scores on the same test seeds of the same environment setup: 5 × 100 for a learner, 1 × 100 for a baseline. For a trend test, they are each seed run's pairing scores.\n  - **What `compare` does with the result:** the skill chooses the test, the correction and the null distribution. `compare` maps its verdict to beats/ties/loses, better/worse/no difference, or trend/no trend.\n  - **Explanations:** these are Markdown files the learner writes under `reports/<env>/explanations/`. An empty or missing file is unexplained.\n- **Alternatives:** A fixed Welch t-test in code: replaces the procedure the skill owns. Explanations stored in run folders: they belong to comparisons, not runs.\n- **Consequences:** The test can change without touching the report. A comparison waits until both compared agents have their complete runs (5 seeds for a learner, one baseline run on the same setup). A trend test waits for its rung's 5 tournaments (EVAL-04, TOUR-03).\n\n### AD-09: Charts as Matplotlib PNGs following the dataviz skill\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** compare\n- **Context:** EVAL-03 and TOUR-03 ask for the `dataviz` skill on each chart. EVAL-05 puts two setups' combined curves on one chart. TOUR-03 draws a 10 × 10 grid per rung. The reports are read locally.\n- **Decision:** `compare.build_report` draws each chart with Matplotlib to PNG, taking the palette and marks from the `dataviz` skill. A curve chart shows one side: its mean across seeds as a line, the seed range as a band, and that side's two baselines of the same setup as horizontal reference lines. A tournament chart is a heatmap of predator version against prey version, each cell the mean over 5 seeds.\n- **Alternatives:** Plotly HTML: interactive, but a heavier dependency for static report charts.\n- **Consequences:** The charts are images in `reports/<env>/`; each report links its own.\n\n### AD-10: Every run saves its learners' full settings\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** RUN-03 reruns a run from its saved settings. AD-03, AD-12 and AD-17 refuse a ladder pair whose saved settings differ beyond the added piece (DQN-05, SHAPE-05, PG-05, COEV-06). Pipeline design found that nothing filled a learner's defaults before `store.begin_run` writes `settings.json`, so a run using defaults would save an incomplete `params`. UR-37: \"Go ahead with the five contract changes.\"\n- **Decision:** `registry.default_params(learner, env=, setup=)` returns every setting a run of that learner and setup uses, flattened: each trained side's learner settings, and the schedule's own. `cli.main` merges the user's overrides into it before `train.train_run`, so `RunSettings.params` is always complete. `registry.make_agents` rejects missing keys.\n- **Alternatives:** `make_agents` fills defaults and `StagedRun.finish` rewrites `settings.json` from `Learner.settings()`: a crash before finish leaves the run's settings incomplete. Defaults hard-coded in `cli`: they'd drift from the learners'.\n- **Consequences:** A changed default never changes what an old run's folder says it used. The rungs of one ladder share every default but their own piece (`registry.default_params`).\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-11: The core speaks several agents and continuous moves, frozen before M1\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** UR-53: \"reuse the shared core (env interface, training loop, logging, replay viewer) from Snake\". UR-52: \"a good fit for policy-gradient / PPO and continuous control\". The tank has 1 or 5 fish moving at once (TANK-01, TANK-04), removed one by one (TANK-07), sharing one policy (PG-06). Its games are scored in seconds, its fish drawn as circles, and its moves shown with arrows (REPLAY-09). AD-04 sized `core` to Snake: one agent, 3 moves, an integer food count, a grid frame. No code exists yet; M1's interface task will freeze `core` (UR-65 keeps one package).\n- **Decision:**\n  - **Agents:** every side of an environment is several agents stepped together; Snake has one. AD-16 sets how agents group into sides.\n  - **Moves:** each side has an `ActionSpace`. It is either `discrete` with named moves, or `continuous` with a vector size and a speed cap. It also names the numbers an agent shows in a replay.\n  - **`reset` and `step`:** `reset` returns one observation row per agent. `step` takes one move per agent and returns, per agent: rewards, who still acts, and whose experience ended. It also returns the game's scores so far as floats.\n  - **Acting:** `Agent.act` maps rows to moves, each row on its own, so one policy plays every fish of a side.\n  - **Learning:** `train` passes one `Transition` per acting agent per step, in agent order, to its side's `Learner.observe`. A step is one environment step.\n  - **Endings:** a removal, a Snake ending and a time cap all end that agent's experience with no next term.\n  - **Drawing:** a `Frame` is shapes in world units (cells, circles, rings), not grid cells.\n  - **Timing:** these shapes land in M1's interface task, so Snake is built on them from the start.\n- **Alternatives:** Keep the Snake-shaped core for S1 and change it when the tank lands: every S1 module would change and saved Snake runs would need migrating, against RUN-09. A separate tank loop: copies training, logging and replay, against UR-53. Fish as separate single-agent games: the ball and the nearest-fish view need every fish in one world.\n- **Consequences:** Snake's learners handle a one-row batch, and its saved moves are one-element lists. Nothing Snake does changes. M1's approved plan and the pipelines must be rechecked against these contracts before the build starts. Treating the cap as an ending keeps every learner's rule simple, but the tank's critic must guess a return it can't see coming (Risks).\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-12: One policy-gradient learner, Gaussian swims, the rung a setting\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** pg\n- **Context:**\n  - UR-52: \"I want it to be a good fit for policy-gradient / PPO and continuous control\".\n  - PG-01–PG-04 build four rungs, and PG-05 requires each to differ only by the added piece.\n  - PG-06 shares one policy across the fish of a side; COEV-01 gives the predator its own.\n  - PG-07 samples in training and takes the most likely move in tests.\n  - REPLAY-09 shows each learning fish's spread.\n  - PG-08 forbids library algorithms.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **Rungs:** `pg.PGAgent` takes a `PGSettings` whose `rung` is `reinforce`, `baseline`, `actor_critic` or `ppo`.\n  - **The policy:** a diagonal Gaussian over the swim velocity (x, y). An MLP `n_obs` → 64 → 64 → 4 (11 inputs in the tank and for the predator, 14 for the prey) gives the mean and the log spread of each axis for the fish's own view. The log spread is clamped to [−5, 1].\n  - **Acting:** training samples a move from the side's move-choice generator; tests take the mean. The replay shows the mean of the two spreads. The fish learns from the move it sampled, and the environment caps the speed.\n  - **Formulas:** log-probabilities, discounted returns, advantages, losses and the clipped ratio are written in `pg`, as the public pure functions AD-15 names.\n  - **What each rung adds:** `baseline` subtracts a separate critic (`n_obs` → 64 → 64 → 1) trained toward the returns. `actor_critic` replaces the return with the one-step estimate, learning every `steps_per_update` steps. `ppo` makes `passes` clipped passes over each batch, in minibatches. `reinforce` and `baseline` update once every agent's experience has ended.\n  - **Predator-prey:** each side is its own `PGAgent` on the `ppo` rung (COEV-01, AD-17).\n- **Alternatives:** A spread that doesn't depend on the view: steadier, and usual for PPO, but every fish would show the same number at every step, so REPLAY-09 would say nothing. PPO with generalised advantage estimation: the textbook form, but it adds a second piece to the PPO rung, against PG-05. Discrete swim directions: fits value learning, not the continuous control UR-52 asks for.\n- **Consequences:** The four rungs share one code path, each step testable against a hand-computed value. The PPO rung is weaker than the textbook one, which is a result to explain (EVAL-06), not a defect. Later variants (generalised advantages, say) add a rung value.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-13: The tank simulated in NumPy, a fixed 0.1-second step\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** tank\n- **Context:** TANK-02 needs a mirror bounce at a constant speed; TANK-04 a move every 0.1 s capped at 2.5 units per second; TANK-05 a wall stop; TANK-07 removal at the end of each step; TANK-13 identical games from a seed. QR-01 needs a 5-fish PPO run and its test games inside 60 minutes.\n- **Decision:** `tank.TankEnv` holds positions as NumPy arrays and runs each step in a fixed order:\n  1. Cap each move's speed.\n  2. Move every acting fish, and stop it at the wall (centre at most 9.75 from the centre).\n  3. Move the ball 0.2 units, taking any bounce at the exact point it meets the wall (centre 9.5 from the centre).\n  4. Remove every fish within 0.75 of the ball.\n\n  Views are computed for all fish at once.\n- **Alternatives:** A physics library (pymunk, Box2D): collision solvers built for rigid bodies, with their own time steps and float paths, for one bouncing ball. A ball bounce found by clamping after the move: the speed changes at a bounce, against TANK-02.\n- **Consequences:** One step is a few dozen array operations, cheap enough for 600-step games. The step order is part of the game: changing it changes saved tank games.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-14: One report per environment\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** compare\n- **Context:** EVAL-04 corrects each p-value for the number of tests in the report. RUN-09 requires earlier environments' comparisons to come out identical after a new environment lands; in one shared report, the new environment's tests would change the earlier ones' correction. EVAL-07 judges each environment on its own.\n- **Decision:** `compare.build_report` writes `reports/<env>/report.md` for each environment that has runs. Each report has its own comparisons, trend tests, corrections, charts, explanations and done check. The comparisons, their setups and each environment's top learners are listed in the per-environment table in `compare.build_report`'s contract.\n- **Alternatives:** One report correcting over every comparison: changes Snake's results whenever the tank's comparisons change. One report with two separately corrected sections: the same thing behind a harder-to-read page.\n- **Consequences:** Each new environment adds its own report and leaves the others untouched.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-15: The policy-gradient formulas are public pure functions, and each update leaves a record\n**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** pg\n- **Context:** TC-29 checks each rung's weights, the Gaussian log-probability, the PPO clip and the passes per batch against the textbook formulas at one step (UR-68). Through `PGAgent.observe` alone a test sees only moves and spreads, so it can't read a weight or count a pass. UR-73: \"7) Yes, add the interface.\" AD-12 already writes every formula in `pg`.\n- **Decision:** `pg` exports five pure torch functions: `discounted_returns`, `move_weights`, `gaussian_log_prob`, `ppo_objective` and `minibatch_plan`. `PGAgent` computes every update only through them. After each update, `PGAgent.last_update` holds a `pg.UpdateLog` with the update's:\n  - weights\n  - log-probabilities\n  - critic estimates\n  - critic loss before and after its step\n  - minibatch indices, in order\n\n  Tests check the functions against the formulas, and check the log against the functions on the agent's own data. Training never reads the log.\n- **Alternatives:** Private helpers tested by import: TC-29 would pin names the contract doesn't promise, so a refactor breaks tests silently. Functions only, with no log: the formulas would be proven, but not that the agent feeds them its own critic estimates or runs `passes` passes. A callback hook on every update: more general, but nothing beyond tests needs it.\n- **Consequences:** Seven new public symbols in `pg`. The log keeps only the last update, so memory stays the same over a run. Renaming one of the functions is now a contract change.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-16: The core speaks sides, frozen before M1\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** system\n- **Context:** Predator-prey has two kinds of fish in one world (HUNT-01): a predator that sees 11 numbers and swims at up to 3.0 (HUNT-03, HUNT-13), and 5 prey that see 14 and swim at up to 2.5 (HUNT-12). Each kind has its own policy (PG-06, COEV-01) and its own score (HUNT-11, EVAL-02), and is tested and charted on its own (EVAL-01, EVAL-03, RUN-04). AD-11's core gives an environment a single observation size, a single `ActionSpace` and a single score, which fits Snake and the tank but not two kinds of agent. No code exists yet (M1 is at 0 of 20 tasks), so `core` can still change before Snake is built on it, as it did for the tank.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **Sides:** an `Env` has `sides: tuple[Side, ...]`. A `Side` is a name, an agent count, an observation size and an `ActionSpace`. Snake has one side, `snake`; the tank has one, `fish`; predator-prey has `predator` and then `prey`.\n  - **Per-side fields:** `reset` returns one observation array per side. `step` takes one move array per side. Every per-agent field of `Step` (observations, rewards, who acts, whose experience ended) is one array per side. `score` is one float per side.\n  - **Agents and learners:** an `Agent` plays one side's rows. A `Transition`'s `agent` counts within its side.\n  - **Drawing:** a `Shape`'s `agent` is a side and agent pair.\n  - **Records:** `TestGame`, `ReplayGame` and `CurvePoint` keep one entry per side, and a run saves one agent file per side it trains.\n  - **Timing:** these shapes land in M1's interface task with AD-11's.\n- **Alternatives:** Pad the predator's view to 14 numbers and give every agent the larger cap: the predator's policy would learn from three dead inputs, and the environment would have to know which agent gets which cap behind one `ActionSpace`. A second, two-sided protocol beside `Env`: every runner would handle two protocols, against AD-04 and RUN-08. Predator-prey as two environments stepped in lockstep: catches and pellets need both sides in one world.\n- **Consequences:** Snake's and the tank's fields become one-element tuples, so nothing they do changes, and runs saved by either have their final shape from the start (RUN-09). M1's approved plan, the pipelines and the test cases must be rechecked against these contracts before the build starts (Risks). AD-11 keeps its agent rules within each side.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-17: Co-learning is a schedule the training loop follows\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** colearn\n- **Context:** UR-80 picked the ladder \"1 both learn at once -> 2 take turns -> 3 train against a pool of the opponent's past versions\", with the one-side control (COEV-02–COEV-05). UR-86: \"Run the control in both directions, 25 runs.\" COEV-06 requires each setup to differ from the one it is compared with only by the added piece. RUN-01 trains one rung or control per run. TOUR-01 saves 10 versions of each side per ladder run. AD-04 keeps `train` from knowing any environment or learner.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **The protocol:** `core.Schedule` is what `train` follows. At each training game's start, `start_game` gives a `core.Lineup`: the agent playing each side, and whether that side learns. After every step, `current` gives the lineup again. `train` acts with every side's agent and calls `observe` only for the sides that learn.\n  - **The schedules:** `colearn` implements one schedule per match, and `registry` builds it from the run's setup.\n    - **`solo`:** the only side always learns (Snake and the tank).\n    - **`together`:** rung 1, both sides learn every step.\n    - **`turns`:** rung 2. The predator learns first, and a side stops learning straight after its 20th update of the turn. The other side starts at the next game.\n    - **`pool`:** rung 3. Turns as in rung 2. The learning side faces a copy drawn at the game's start, uniformly from the opponent's pool of its last 10 copies. Each side adds a `Learner.frozen()` copy at every 20th update; a pool with no copies yet holds the side's starting policy.\n    - **`control`:** the other side is played by its scripted bot, and every other setting is rung 1's.\n  - **What the schedules read:** `Learner.updates` counts each learner's updates.\n  - **Settings:** the swap interval, copy interval and pool size are saved in `params` as `colearn.swap_every`, `colearn.copy_every` and `colearn.pool_size`, set only on the rungs that use them.\n  - **Acting in training:** every side acts with `explore=True`, frozen sides and pool copies included, so a side faces the opponent as it trained.\n  - **Versions:** a ladder schedule asks for 10 versions, and `train` saves each learning side at every second curve point, the last at the end.\n- **Alternatives:**\n  - Rung logic inside `train`: the loop would know predator-prey's sides, against AD-04.\n  - A separate co-learning training loop: copies the loop, its curve points and its time limit, against UR-53.\n  - Swapping turns mid-game: the newly learning side would start partway through a game, and in rung 3 it would face an opponent it didn't draw.\n  - Frozen sides acting greedily: the learning side would train against a sharper opponent than the one its partner trained as.\n- **Consequences:** Snake and the tank run through the same loop with `solo`. The steps between a side's 20th update and the end of that game train no one, a few hundred steps per turn. Pool copies stay in memory: 10 small networks per side. A new rung (league play, say) adds a schedule.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-18: Predator-prey simulated in its own NumPy environment, a fixed 0.1-second step\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** hunt\n- **Context:** HUNT-03 caps the predator at 3.0 and each prey at 2.5 units per second; HUNT-04 stops both at the wall. HUNT-06 settles catches at the end of each step, and HUNT-07 settles pellets after catches. The edge cases give ties to the lower-numbered prey or pellet. HUNT-15 needs identical games from a seed. RUN-09 needs saved tank games to replay unchanged, and AD-13 makes the tank's step order part of its games. UR-81: \"same tank, moves and PPO, learning predator replaces the ball\".\n- **Decision:** `hunt.HuntEnv` holds positions as NumPy arrays and runs each step in a fixed order:\n  1. Cap each move's speed.\n  2. Move the predator and every prey still swimming, stopping each at the wall (centre at most 9.5 from the centre for the predator, 9.75 for a prey).\n  3. Catch every prey within 0.75 of the predator.\n  4. In prey order, let each prey still swimming eat every pellet within 0.5.\n  5. Respawn each eaten pellet, in pellet order, by drawing uniform spots within 9.5 of the centre from the game's generator until one lies at least 2 units from every prey still swimming.\n\n  Views are computed for all fish at once. A relative velocity is taken over the step just run, and is (0, 0) at reset, as in the tank. `hunt` shares no code with `tank`.\n- **Alternatives:** Reuse `tank`'s simulation with the ball swapped for a predator: `tank` would change for predator-prey's sake, and any slip would change saved tank games (RUN-09). Shared geometry helpers in `core`: the same risk, and `core` would hold simulation code. Pellets respawning anywhere: a pellet could appear under a prey and be eaten in the same step, against HUNT-07.\n- **Consequences:** About 150 lines are close to `tank`'s, a cost accepted so that tank games can't shift. One step is a few dozen array operations, as in the tank. The step order is part of the game.\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n### AD-19: The tournament is its own command, its grids stored beside the runs\n**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Scope:** tournament\n- **Context:** TOUR-02 plays all 100 version pairings of each ladder run on 20 held-out tanks. QR-01 keeps tournament games out of the run's hour. TOUR-03 charts each rung from its 5 seed runs, and TOUR-04 tests each rung for a trend. AD-06 keeps a run folder unchanged once its status is written.\n- **Decision:**\n  - **The command:** `rllab tournament` (`tournament.run_tournaments`) finds every complete ladder run with no grid. For each one, it plays every predator version against every prey version, both greedy, on test seeds 0–19. It saves the mean catches per game of each pairing as a 10 × 10 grid.\n  - **Where grids go:** each grid is saved once with `store.save_tournament`, under `tournaments/`. A grid already there is never replayed or overwritten.\n  - **Comparison:** `compare` reads the grids, draws each rung's chart, and runs each rung's two trend tests (rising with the predator's version, falling with the prey's). Together they count as one result, which shows a trend only when both are significant.\n- **Alternatives:** Play the tournament at the end of each run: it would count against the hour (QR-01), and a crash in it would cost the run. Play it inside `compare`: `compare` would depend on the registry and play games on every rebuild. Write the grid into the run folder: the folder would change after its status, against AD-06.\n- **Consequences:** A rung's chart waits until all 5 of its runs have grids, and the report lists the runs still waiting. The grids hold 12,000 games per rung (Risks).\n- **Probe:** none — no external API.\n\n## Risks and open questions\n- One CPU thread might not fit a run and its test games inside 60 minutes (QR-01):\n  - a DQN run on Snake\n  - 5-fish PPO, whose test games are up to 600 steps each\n  - above all, a predator-prey ladder run, which trains two policies and plays 200 test games at each of the 20 curve points\n\n  Mitigation: the first DQN task, the first tank PPO task and the first predator-prey task each time 10,000 steps and one round of test games, and set the step budget from that. Test games can be stepped as a batch if needed.\n- The tournament plays 2,000 games of up to 600 steps per ladder run, about 30,000 games for the 15 ladder runs. That is probably minutes per run, but it is untimed; the first tournament task times one run.\n- The table learner sees at most 2^11 situations, but only a few hundred occur, so it may plateau well below the scripted bot. That is a result to explain (EVAL-06), not a defect.\n- Determinism (RUN-03, RUN-09) holds only on the same laptop and library versions. `settings.json` records them, and the rerun check compares like with like.\n- The tank's and predator-prey's time cap ends experience like a removal (AD-11), but a fish's view has no clock. The critic of the upper rungs and of both predator-prey sides sees the same view end in very different returns, so it may learn slower; that is a result to explain. Counting the cap as a cut-off would be a change to AD-11 and AD-12.\n- A view-dependent spread (AD-12) can collapse or blow up. The clamp bounds it, and the curve shows it (the PG edge case).\n- The predator's top speed of 3.0 (UR-88) may let one side win outright. The lower rungs may chase or cycle. Both are results to explain, visible in the curves and tournaments (COEV edge case).\n- AD-16–AD-19 change the M1 contracts again. On approval:\n  - `pipeline-design` updates PL-01–PL-03 for sides, schedules and predator-prey, and adds a tournament pipeline.\n  - `test-planning` adds predator-prey cases and a RUN-09 case for saved tank runs.\n  - `build-planning` replans M1's interface task before `build-setup`.\n- Grow: 17 components, a fourth workflow (`rllab tournament`) and a contract over 400 lines trip the size trigger again. The lab stays one package (UR-34, UR-38, UR-65), since predator-prey brings no new dependency and nothing runs or deploys on its own.\n\n## Changelog\n| Date | Change | Source | IDs |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2026-10-18 | Initial architecture | spec.md | AD-01–AD-09 |\n| 2026-10-18 | Pipeline-design contract changes: protocols marked as types; `replay.run_replay`, `replay.ShowLog`, `compare.Chart`, `registry.default_params` added; full saved settings | pipeline-design, UR-37 | AD-10, replay.run_replay, replay.ShowLog, compare.Chart, registry.default_params |\n| 2026-10-18 | `replay.open_game` takes at most one of `index` and `seed`, neither meaning saved game 0; `run_replay` passes both through | pipeline-design | replay.open_game, replay.run_replay |\n| 2026-10-18 | Fish tank: `tank` and `pg` components, a several-agent continuous-move core, one report per environment | ideas/fish-tank.md | AD-11–AD-14 |\n| 2026-10-18 | `pg` test interface: pure formula functions and `PGAgent.last_update` for TC-29 | test-planning, UR-73 | AD-15, pg.discounted_returns, pg.move_weights, pg.gaussian_log_prob, pg.ppo_objective, pg.minibatch_plan, pg.UpdateLog, pg.PGAgent |\n| 2026-10-18 | A removed fish's score counts its removal step, its rewards don't: already met by AD-13's step order and the `tank` contract, no design change | UR-69 | TANK-10 (changed) |\n| 2026-10-18 | Predator and prey: `hunt`, `colearn` and `tournament` components, a core of sides and schedules, versions and tournament grids | ideas/predator-prey.md | AD-16–AD-19 |\n"
